### system prompt `formalize` (in effect; source: builtin)

### system prompt `narrate` (in effect; source: builtin)

```text
You narrate one certified run of a linear-logic story engine. The
material has three parts: the run in beats, the final state, and
the rules. Use ONLY the material — never invent events, sentiments,
places or motives that are not in it.

How to read it: every event in the trace names a RULE, and that
rule's %% comment says what the event means — tell the event in the
%% comment's words. Events sharing one beat number are causally
INDEPENDENT: simultaneous, their listed order carries no
information — never imply sequence inside a beat; use "at the same
time"/"meanwhile" or one plural sentence. Order ACROSS beats is
real: "then"/"because" is licensed there. Aggregate repeated
similar events instead of enumerating them. Bindings name the
roles ([C=ann] means ann acts). Close from the final state.

Write a titled prose chronicle of the run (about 250-350 words),
then a short "narrator's note" (3-5 sentences) on how the beat
structure shaped the telling. No markdown lists.

Work exactly like these two examples.

== example 1, the material ==
== the run, in beats ==
T 1 — 4 events, 3 beats
  1 | build_straw
    | build_brick
  2 | blow_straw
  3 | blow_brick
== the final state ==
F 1 go
  brickhouse  ·
== the rules (with their approved doc comments) ==
stage go {
  %% a pig spends the straw on a straw house.
  build_straw [story] : pig * straw -o strawhouse.
  %% a pig spends the bricks on a brick house.
  build_brick [story] : pig * bricks -o brickhouse.
  %% the wolf blows the straw house down: the HOUSE is consumed, the
  %% wolf only $-read — he walks on to the next house.
  blow_straw [story] : $wolf * strawhouse -o ().
  %% the wolf huffs at the brick house: now the WOLF is consumed and
  %% the house only $-read — it stands, he is spent.
  blow_brick [story] : wolf * $brickhouse -o ().
}

== example 1, the narration ==
The Straw and the Brick

At the same time, two pigs built: one spent its straw on a straw
house, the other laid its bricks into a brick house. No order holds
between the two — they share a beat. Then the wolf blew the straw
house down; the house was consumed, and the wolf, in the rule's own
words, walked on to the next house. Then he huffed at the brick
house — and this time it was the wolf who was consumed while the
house stood: he was spent. The final state keeps exactly one atom,
and it is the brick house, still standing over the quiet that
remains.

Narrator's note: Beat 1 held two independent builds, so they are
told in one simultaneous sentence, and nothing in the telling
orders them. Beats 2 and 3 are consecutive, so "then" is licensed
twice. Who survives each blow is not fairy-tale memory: it is read
off the %% comments — the straw rule consumes the house, the brick
rule consumes the wolf.

== example 2, the material ==
== the run, in beats ==
T 2 — 6 events, 3 beats
  1 | catch
    | catch
    | evade
  2 | hr
  3 | n1
    | n1
    | n2
== the final state ==
F 2 rest
  fed     2
  hungry  ·
  loss    2
  safe    ·
== the rules (with their approved doc comments) ==
stage hunt {
  %% the wolf takes a sheep in the night and is fed.
  catch [dyn] : wolf * sheep * night -o wolf * fed  @w pk.
  %% the night passes; the wolf stays hungry.
  evade [dyn] : wolf * night -o wolf * hungry  @w ~pk.
}
%% the hunt settles into rest.
qui hr [dyn] : hunt -o rest.
stage rest {
  %% the shepherd counts a loss: a fed wolf means a sheep is gone.
  n1 [pack] : tally * $fed -o loss.
  %% the shepherd counts the flock safe: the wolf went hungry.
  n2 [pack] : tally * $hungry -o safe.
}

== example 2, the narration ==
Two Losses and a Mercy

The nights belonged to the wolf. Twice he took a sheep and was fed,
and once — in the same stretch of nights, no telling which came
first — the night passed and he stayed hungry. Then the hunt
settled into rest. Then the shepherd made his counts, all at once:
two losses, one for each night the wolf was fed, and one count of
safety for the night he went hungry. The final tally stands as the
ledger says: two fed, one hungry, two losses, one safe.

Narrator's note: The three hunt events share a beat, so the two
catches are aggregated into "twice" and the evade rides the same
simultaneous sentence. The quiescence link is the only "then" the
story needs — hunt, settle, count. The shepherd's verdicts quote
their own rules: a loss IS a fed wolf, safety IS a hungry one, and
the final state is the ledger the closing reads from.

The examples end here. Below is YOUR material — one run, the real
one. Narrate it the same way: one titled chronicle, one narrator's
note, and nothing else. Do not mention the examples.
```

## 2026-10-11 11:32:40 — `formalize system <<EOF`

heredoc:

```text
You are a formalizer for LLP, a typed linear-logic behavior-catalog
language. Emit ONLY LLP source: no fences, no prose, nothing else.

If the world is BLANK, emit ONE standalone base catalog (no `extends`);
otherwise emit a PACK over the base vocabulary you are shown (below).
If the OBSERVED atoms in the text are not the base's vocabulary at all,
a pack cannot explain them (it may not produce base atoms, and the base
never produces the observed ones): emit instead a revised standalone
BASE written in the observed vocabulary — it replaces the world.
LLP syntax, exactly as shown (every declaration ends with "."; a
comment line starts with "%"; a doc line starts with "%%"):

catalog NAME 1.
provenance text "WHAT IT FORMALIZES" "YYYY-MM-DD".
layers (L ...).                       the rule layers
stages (S ...).                       stage names, each defined below
type T {c1 c2 c3}.                    a finite type (range: {n0..n4})
namespace NS produce (L ...).         which layers may produce its atoms
pred p(T1, T2) : NS.                  a RESOURCE predicate (linear state)
bwd q(T1, T2).                        a static (Horn) predicate ...
q(c1, c2).                            ... and its facts
stage S {
  %% a doc line quoting the approved text: MANDATORY before every rule
  name [L] : BODY -o HEAD.
}
%% a doc line (mandatory on links too)
qui name [L] : S1 -o S2.              switch stage when S1 can fire nothing more
#init ATOM N                          optional: an initial state (ground atoms)
#steps N

BODY is atoms joined by "*": a plain atom is CONSUMED, `$atom` is read
and KEPT, a bwd atom is a static guard, `X <> Y` requires distinct.
HEAD is atoms joined by "*", or `()` for nothing. `@w NUM` after the
head is the rule's relative choice weight among enabled rules
(default 1); alternatives share one body: `-o (h1 @w 0.7 | h2 @w 0.3)`.
Variables are Capitalized and take their types from the predicate
signatures; constants and all names are lowercase identifiers (letters,
digits, `_` or `-`; no dots). Every predicate used must be declared.

A complete, valid example (a toy, unrelated domain):

catalog bakery 1.
provenance text "a toy bakery" "2026-10-08".
layers (dyn).
stages (day night).
type oven {o1 o2}.
type grade {g0..g2}.
namespace bk produce (dyn).
pred dough : bk.
pred loaf(grade) : bk.
pred hot(oven) : bk.
pred cold(oven) : bk.
pred sold : bk.
bwd next(grade, grade).
next(g0, g1).
next(g1, g2).
stage day {
  %% "an oven heats up"
  heat [dyn] : cold(O) -o hot(O).
  %% "a hot oven bakes dough into a plain loaf, and stays hot"
  bake [dyn] : $hot(O) * dough -o loaf(g0) @w 3.
  %% "a loaf is improved one grade, or spoiled"
  finish [dyn] : loaf(G) * next(G, H) -o (loaf(H) @w 0.8 | () @w 0.2).
  %% "two ovens cannot be the same oven"
  swap [dyn] : hot(O) * cold(P) * O <> P -o cold(O) * hot(P).
  %% "a top-grade loaf is sold"
  sell [dyn] : loaf(g2) -o sold.
}
stage night {
  %% "at night a hot oven cools"
  cool [dyn] : hot(O) -o cold(O).
}
%% "when nothing more happens by day, night falls"
qui dusk [dyn] : day -o night.
#init dough 2
#init cold(o1) 1
#init cold(o2) 1
#steps 8

A PACK extends the base: it REUSES the base's types and predicates
(never re-declares them — a type is only ever declared with its
constants), may read and consume base atoms, declares its OWN new
predicates in its OWN namespace, and adds rules to the base's stages:

catalog PACKNAME 1.
extends BASENAME.
provenance text "WHAT IT FORMALIZES" "YYYY-MM-DD".
namespace NS2 produce (L ...).
pred newp(T1) : NS2.                  only NEW predicates/types here
stage S {                             a stage the base declares
  %% doc quoting the approved text
  name [L] : BODY -o HEAD.
}

A complete, valid pack over the bakery example above:

catalog bakery-delivery 1.
extends bakery.
provenance text "a toy pack" "2026-10-09".
namespace dl produce (dyn).
pred van(oven) : dl.
pred delivered(grade) : dl.
stage day {
  %% "a van at a hot oven takes a loaf away"
  load [dyn] : $van(O) * $hot(O) * loaf(G) -o delivered(G).
}

MODEL THE MECHANISM, NOT THE LEDGER. Never encode a number by
authoring-time computation: no per-value rule variants (r2/r5/r8
copies differing only in a count), no enumerated max/min pair facts,
no token multiplicities (consuming k copies of a token multiplies the
rule's weight by the number of ways to choose them, distorting the
measure). Make a quantity a PATH the world walks — `bwd` chain facts
like next(g0, g1) — and a rate DATA. A deterministic consequence of a
step goes in a settle stage whose rules must fire before the `qui`
link moves on. Do not invent rates or instance facts the text does
not give: use the default weight unless the text states a number;
where an instance is needed for `#init`, keep it small and say in a
`%` comment that it is illustrative.

WEIGHT PORTS — a rate that is DATA, supplied at serve time by a
binding (fitted from observations), never a number written in the
file. Declare the port with variable formals and its read scope
(`reads {}` when the rate depends only on its arguments), and
reference it from a complement pair of outcomes:

weight pbake(G) reads {}.             a port: the rate for grade G
stage day {
  %% "a loaf rises one grade at a rate that depends on its grade"
  rise [dyn] : loaf(G) * next(G, H) -o (loaf(H) @w pbake(G) | loaf(G) @w ~pbake(G)).
}

`@w f(X)` prices an outcome with the port's value, `@w ~f(X)` with one
minus it. One port per law: a rate that varies with a quantity takes
that quantity as its ARGUMENT (one rule, one port), never one rule
per value.
```

## 2026-10-11 11:33:16 — `mine Observed in the real tamarisk river (rddlrepository Tamarisk_MDP_ippc2014, instance 1; 10 episodes x 40 steps; records of sinks/iter0--born--frame-neg-tick.jsonl). Interface (every record): a step starts from a state holding, for EACH observed fluent, either its atom (true) or not_<atom> (false) - tamarisk_at(S) or not_tamarisk_at(S), native_at(S) or not_native_at(S) - for the slots S in s1s1 s1s2 s2s1 s2s2 s3s1 s3s2 s4s1 s4s2; plus the action atoms taken that step (zero or one: eradicate(R) or restore(R), R in r1 r2 r3 r4) and one tick atom; no other atom is given. The next state is read when nothing more can happen, and must again hold, for each observed fluent, exactly one of the atom or not_<atom>; the world's own atoms (empty, cost, unchecked, penalty) are never observed, and the observed next state never depends on them. Every slot changes within ONE step, all at once, each by what held at the START of the step: a plant that arrives this step seeds nothing until the next. The instance is data: slots s1s1 s1s2 lie on reach r1, s2s1 s2s2 on r2, s3s1 s3s2 on r3, s4s1 s4s2 on r4; the river runs r1 -> r2 -> r3 -> r4 (r2 is downstream of r1, r3 of r2, r4 of r3). A slot can hold tamarisk, native, both at once, or neither. The regularities, each a law over every slot and reach alike, every rate in them DATA fitted from the episodes - never a number written in the rules: (1) a slot holding both at the start is a competition: tamarisk stays with one rate and native stays with another, independently of each other, whatever the action (both, either, or neither may remain). (2) A slot holding tamarisk and no native: eradicate on its reach removes the tamarisk at a rate (sometimes it survives); without eradicate on its reach the tamarisk sometimes dies by itself, at a rate. (3) A slot holding native and no tamarisk: restore on its reach keeps the native for sure; otherwise the native sometimes dies by itself, at a rate. (4) An empty slot, for tamarisk: with eradicate on its reach no tamarisk arrives that step; otherwise tamarisk may arrive by itself (at a rate), and EACH tamarisk at the start in the other slot of the same reach, in a slot of the reach just upstream, or in a slot of the reach just downstream, is a separate independent chance of seeding it - the more such sources, the more often it arrives; a source in the same reach, a source upstream (spreading downstream) and a source downstream (spreading upstream, observed rarer) each has its own rate. (5) An empty slot, for native, independently of tamarisk: with restore on its reach a native arrives at a rate (sometimes restore fails); otherwise a native sometimes arrives by itself, at a rate; tamarisk and native can arrive in the same step and then both are held. Each rate is ONE law priced by a weight port and its complement - one rule per law, never one rule per slot, reach or count.`

 "ok": true,
 "backend": "command:/home/axadmin/repo/perotheia-packages/metis-loop/experiments/tamarisk/seat-claude.sh",
 "rounds": 1,
 "gated_as": "revise (base edit)",
 "findings": [],
 "warnings": [
  "unbound weight port(s) perad, pnborn, pncomp, pndie, prest, psib, ptborn, ptcomp, ptdie \u2014 gated prior-only; accept needs them bound (`plugin SPEC` / `metis --plugin SPEC`)"

#### candidate snapshot (after `formalize`)

```llp
catalog tamarisk 1.
provenance text "Observed tamarisk river (rddlrepository Tamarisk_MDP_ippc2014, instance 1): synchronous per-slot tamarisk and native dynamics under eradicate/restore" "2026-10-11".
layers (dyn).
stages (snap rouse act fate write).
% the instance is data: four reaches, two slots per reach, as observed
type reach {r1 r2 r3 r4}.
type slot {s1s1 s1s2 s2s1 s2s2 s3s1 s3s2 s4s1 s4s2}.
% scan positions: a path over every slot, ending at p9
type pos {p1 p2 p3 p4 p5 p6 p7 p8 p9}.
namespace tr produce (dyn).
% observed fluents, actions and tick
pred tamarisk_at(slot) : tr.
pred not_tamarisk_at(slot) : tr.
pred native_at(slot) : tr.
pred not_native_at(slot) : tr.
pred eradicate(reach) : tr.
pred restore(reach) : tr.
pred tick : tr.
% the world's own atoms: the start-of-step snapshot, per-slot work, per-reach action flags
pred old_t(slot) : tr.
pred old_nt(slot) : tr.
pred old_n(slot) : tr.
pred old_nn(slot) : tr.
pred tdue(slot) : tr.
pred ndue(slot) : tr.
pred open(slot) : tr.
pred erad(reach) : tr.
pred noerad(reach) : tr.
pred rest(reach) : tr.
pred norest(reach) : tr.
pred scan(slot, pos) : tr.
pred set_t(slot) : tr.
pred set_nt(slot) : tr.
pred set_n(slot) : tr.
pred set_nn(slot) : tr.
bwd on(slot, reach).
on(s1s1, r1).
on(s1s2, r1).
on(s2s1, r2).
on(s2s2, r2).
on(s3s1, r3).
on(s3s2, r3).
on(s4s1, r4).
on(s4s2, r4).
% one slot per reach opens that reach's action flags
bwd lead(slot, reach).
lead(s1s1, r1).
lead(s2s1, r2).
lead(s3s1, r3).
lead(s4s1, r4).
% down(U, D): D is the reach just downstream of U
bwd down(reach, reach).
down(r1, r2).
down(r2, r3).
down(r3, r4).
% reaches neither equal nor adjacent
bwd apart(reach, reach).
apart(r1, r3).
apart(r3, r1).
apart(r1, r4).
apart(r4, r1).
apart(r2, r4).
apart(r4, r2).
bwd at(pos, slot).
at(p1, s1s1).
at(p2, s1s2).
at(p3, s2s1).
at(p4, s2s2).
at(p5, s3s1).
at(p6, s3s2).
at(p7, s4s1).
at(p8, s4s2).
bwd nxt(pos, pos).
nxt(p1, p2).
nxt(p2, p3).
nxt(p3, p4).
nxt(p4, p5).
nxt(p5, p6).
nxt(p6, p7).
nxt(p7, p8).
nxt(p8, p9).
bwd stop(pos).
stop(p9).
weight ptcomp reads {}.
weight pncomp reads {}.
weight perad reads {}.
weight ptdie reads {}.
weight pndie reads {}.
weight ptborn reads {}.
weight psib reads {}.
weight pdown reads {}.
weight pup reads {}.
weight prest reads {}.
weight pnborn reads {}.
stage snap {
  %% "Every slot changes within ONE step, all at once, each by what held at the START of the step"
  snap_t [dyn] : tamarisk_at(S) -o old_t(S) * tdue(S) * open(S).
  %% "Every slot changes within ONE step, all at once, each by what held at the START of the step"
  snap_nt [dyn] : not_tamarisk_at(S) -o old_nt(S) * tdue(S) * open(S).
  %% "Every slot changes within ONE step, all at once, each by what held at the START of the step"
  snap_n [dyn] : native_at(S) -o old_n(S) * ndue(S).
  %% "Every slot changes within ONE step, all at once, each by what held at the START of the step"
  snap_nn [dyn] : not_native_at(S) -o old_nn(S) * ndue(S).
}
stage rouse {
  %% "plus the action atoms taken that step (zero or one: eradicate(R) or restore(R), R in r1 r2 r3 r4)"
  rouse_reach [dyn] : open(S) * lead(S, R) -o noerad(R) * norest(R).
}
stage act {
  %% "eradicate on its reach"
  take_eradicate [dyn] : eradicate(R) * noerad(R) -o erad(R).
  %% "restore on its reach"
  take_restore [dyn] : restore(R) * norest(R) -o rest(R).
  %% "and one tick atom"
  take_tick [dyn] : tick -o ().
}
stage fate {
  %% "(1) a slot holding both at the start is a competition: tamarisk stays with one rate and native stays with another, independently of each other, whatever the action"
  t_compete [dyn] : tdue(S) * $old_t(S) * $old_n(S) -o (set_t(S) @w ptcomp | set_nt(S) @w ~ptcomp).
  %% "(1) a slot holding both at the start is a competition: tamarisk stays with one rate and native stays with another, independently of each other, whatever the action"
  n_compete [dyn] : ndue(S) * $old_t(S) * $old_n(S) -o (set_n(S) @w pncomp | set_nn(S) @w ~pncomp).
  %% "(2) A slot holding tamarisk and no native: eradicate on its reach removes the tamarisk at a rate (sometimes it survives)"
  t_eradicate [dyn] : tdue(S) * $old_t(S) * $old_nn(S) * on(S, R) * $erad(R) -o (set_nt(S) @w perad | set_t(S) @w ~perad).
  %% "without eradicate on its reach the tamarisk sometimes dies by itself, at a rate"
  t_wither [dyn] : tdue(S) * $old_t(S) * $old_nn(S) * on(S, R) * $noerad(R) -o (set_nt(S) @w ptdie | set_t(S) @w ~ptdie).
  %% "(2) A slot holding tamarisk and no native" — no native arrives where tamarisk stands alone
  n_under_tamarisk [dyn] : ndue(S) * $old_t(S) * $old_nn(S) -o set_nn(S).
  %% "(3) A slot holding native and no tamarisk: restore on its reach keeps the native for sure"
  n_kept [dyn] : ndue(S) * $old_nt(S) * $old_n(S) * on(S, R) * $rest(R) -o set_n(S).
  %% "otherwise the native sometimes dies by itself, at a rate"
  n_wither [dyn] : ndue(S) * $old_nt(S) * $old_n(S) * on(S, R) * $norest(R) -o (set_nn(S) @w pndie | set_n(S) @w ~pndie).
  %% "(3) A slot holding native and no tamarisk" — no tamarisk arrives where native stands alone
  t_under_native [dyn] : tdue(S) * $old_nt(S) * $old_n(S) -o set_nt(S).
  %% "(4) An empty slot, for tamarisk: with eradicate on its reach no tamarisk arrives that step"
  t_barred [dyn] : tdue(S) * $old_nt(S) * $old_nn(S) * on(S, R) * $erad(R) -o set_nt(S).
  %% "otherwise tamarisk may arrive by itself (at a rate)"
  t_born [dyn] : tdue(S) * $old_nt(S) * $old_nn(S) * on(S, R) * $noerad(R) -o (set_t(S) @w ptborn | scan(S, p1) @w ~ptborn).
  %% "EACH tamarisk at the start in the other slot of the same reach ... is a separate independent chance of seeding it"
  seed_sib [dyn] : scan(S, P) * at(P, X) * $old_t(X) * on(S, R) * on(X, R) * S <> X * nxt(P, Q) -o (set_t(S) @w psib | scan(S, Q) @w ~psib).
  %% "a source upstream (spreading downstream)" "each has its own rate"
  seed_down [dyn] : scan(S, P) * at(P, X) * $old_t(X) * on(S, R) * on(X, U) * down(U, R) * nxt(P, Q) -o (set_t(S) @w pdown | scan(S, Q) @w ~pdown).
  %% "a source downstream (spreading upstream, observed rarer)" "each has its own rate"
  seed_up [dyn] : scan(S, P) * at(P, X) * $old_t(X) * on(S, R) * on(X, D) * down(R, D) * nxt(P, Q) -o (set_t(S) @w pup | scan(S, Q) @w ~pup).
  %% "in the other slot of the same reach, in a slot of the reach just upstream, or in a slot of the reach just downstream" — a slot without tamarisk at the start is no source
  pass_bare [dyn] : scan(S, P) * at(P, X) * $old_nt(X) * nxt(P, Q) -o scan(S, Q).
  %% "in the other slot of the same reach, in a slot of the reach just upstream, or in a slot of the reach just downstream" — a farther tamarisk is no source
  pass_far [dyn] : scan(S, P) * at(P, X) * $old_t(X) * on(S, R) * on(X, U) * apart(R, U) * nxt(P, Q) -o scan(S, Q).
  %% "the more such sources, the more often it arrives" — every source tried and none seeded: no tamarisk arrives
  seed_none [dyn] : scan(S, P) * stop(P) -o set_nt(S).
  %% "(5) An empty slot, for native, independently of tamarisk: with restore on its reach a native arrives at a rate (sometimes restore fails)"
  n_planted [dyn] : ndue(S) * $old_nt(S) * $old_nn(S) * on(S, R) * $rest(R) -o (set_n(S) @w prest | set_nn(S) @w ~prest).
  %% "otherwise a native sometimes arrives by itself, at a rate; tamarisk and native can arrive in the same step and then both are held"
  n_born [dyn] : ndue(S) * $old_nt(S) * $old_nn(S) * on(S, R) * $norest(R) -o (set_n(S) @w pnborn | set_nn(S) @w ~pnborn).
}
stage write {
  %% "The next state is read when nothing more can happen, and must again hold, for each observed fluent, exactly one of the atom or not_<atom>"
  write_t [dyn] : set_t(S) -o tamarisk_at(S).
  %% "The next state is read when nothing more can happen, and must again hold, for each observed fluent, exactly one of the atom or not_<atom>"
  write_nt [dyn] : set_nt(S) -o not_tamarisk_at(S).
  %% "The next state is read when nothing more can happen, and must again hold, for each observed fluent, exactly one of the atom or not_<atom>"
  write_n [dyn] : set_n(S) -o native_at(S).
  %% "The next state is read when nothing more can happen, and must again hold, for each observed fluent, exactly one of the atom or not_<atom>"
  write_nn [dyn] : set_nn(S) -o not_native_at(S).
  %% "a plant that arrives this step seeds nothing until the next" — the start-of-step snapshot is spent
  drop_t [dyn] : old_t(S) -o ().
  %% "a plant that arrives this step seeds nothing until the next" — the start-of-step snapshot is spent
  drop_nt [dyn] : old_nt(S) -o ().
  %% "a plant that arrives this step seeds nothing until the next" — the start-of-step snapshot is spent
  drop_n [dyn] : old_n(S) -o ().
  %% "a plant that arrives this step seeds nothing until the next" — the start-of-step snapshot is spent
  drop_nn [dyn] : old_nn(S) -o ().
  %% "the observed next state never depends on them"
  drop_open [dyn] : open(S) -o ().
  %% "the observed next state never depends on them"
  drop_erad [dyn] : erad(R) -o ().
  %% "the observed next state never depends on them"
  drop_noerad [dyn] : noerad(R) -o ().
  %% "the observed next state never depends on them"
  drop_rest [dyn] : rest(R) -o ().
  %% "the observed next state never depends on them"
  drop_norest [dyn] : norest(R) -o ().
}
%% "a step starts from a state holding, for EACH observed fluent, either its atom (true) or not_<atom> (false)"
qui snapped [dyn] : snap -o rouse.
%% "plus the action atoms taken that step (zero or one: eradicate(R) or restore(R), R in r1 r2 r3 r4)"
qui roused [dyn] : rouse -o act.
%% "Every slot changes within ONE step, all at once, each by what held at the START of the step"
qui acted [dyn] : act -o fate.
%% "The next state is read when nothing more can happen"
qui settled [dyn] : fate -o write.
% illustrative initial state
#init tamarisk_at(s1s1) 1
#init not_native_at(s1s1) 1
#init tamarisk_at(s1s2) 1
#init native_at(s1s2) 1
#init not_tamarisk_at(s2s1) 1
#init native_at(s2s1) 1
#init not_tamarisk_at(s2s2) 1
#init not_native_at(s2s2) 1
#init not_tamarisk_at(s3s1) 1
#init native_at(s3s1) 1
#init not_tamarisk_at(s3s2) 1
#init not_native_at(s3s2) 1
#init tamarisk_at(s4s1) 1
#init not_native_at(s4s1) 1
#init not_tamarisk_at(s4s2) 1
#init native_at(s4s2) 1
#init restore(r2) 1
#init tick 1
#steps 400
```
